# Telemetry Coverage Contract

This page enumerates every observable stage in the Eshu data plane and the
metric, span, or log key it must emit. It is the single source of truth that
the CI coverage script (X2) diffs against. A new pipeline stage added to the
source tree without a corresponding entry here fails the coverage gate. The
five evidence markers policy (`Performance Evidence:`, `Benchmark Evidence:`,
`No-Regression Evidence:`, `Observability Evidence:`, `No-Observability-Change:`)
at `docs/internal/agent-guide.md:120-146` remains the per-PR discipline; this
doc makes that discipline machine-enforced. Historical precedent lives in
[#3633](https://github.com/eshu-hq/eshu/issues/3633) (closed 2026-06-23),
which proved defined-but-never-registered instruments are a real failure
class; in-flight adoption is [#3680](https://github.com/eshu-hq/eshu/issues/3680)
(open, 2026-06-24), which lands per-collector envelope telemetry at the
shared claimed-service dispatch seam. Metric names match
`go/internal/telemetry/instruments.go`; dimensions, span names, and log keys
match `go/internal/telemetry/contract.go` and its `contract_*.go` siblings.
The public operator contract is `docs/public/reference/telemetry/index.md`.

## How To Read This Doc

- The table is machine-parseable: stable column order
  `stage | file:line | required metric name(s) | category`.
- One row per stage. The metric column lists every registered name an operator
  needs to read at 3 AM, comma-separated. The category column names the owning
  sub-package or seam.
- `file:line` points at the Go file that emits the signal, not the contract.
  When the source spans several call sites, it points at the dispatch chokepoint.
- The literal string `No-Observability-Change:` in a row's metric column is a
  documented decision that the stage does not need a new metric because an
  existing one already diagnoses it.
- The script (X2) grep-parses this table; do not rename columns or insert
  prose between rows in a section.

<!-- eshu:metric:section=reducer-stages -->
## Reducer Stages

The reducer drains queue work items through the worker pool, projects shared
edges, and writes the canonical graph. Each row maps one stage to the metric
or marker that already diagnoses it.

| stage | file:line | required metric name(s) | category |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| intent enqueue | go/internal/projector/runtime.go:173 | `eshu_dp_reducer_intents_enqueued_total` | reducer runtime |

| reducer fact writer primitives | go/internal/reducer/factwrite/writerprims.go | `No-Observability-Change:` Two pure functions that compute no state and perform no I/O: the UTC-normalized write timestamp and the normalized collector-kind column value a fact writer stamps on its rows. Behavior-preserving hoist out of workload_identity_writer.go (#6061); the root keeps both under their original names, so no caller changed. The batched write path these values feed is untouched, so its existing coverage still applies: `eshu_dp_postgres_query_duration_seconds` times the INSERT the row lands in, and `eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total` / `eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds` bound the reducer run around it -- the same trio the sibling factwrite rows cite. An operator diagnosing a bad observed_at or collector_kind value reads the row in Postgres, not a metric, because neither function branches on anything an instrument could record. | reducer fact write |
| reducer canonical single-row fact upsert | go/internal/reducer/factwrite/single_insert.go | `No-Observability-Change:` SingleInsertQuery, the canonical single-row upsert every reducer-owned fact writer uses, extracted unchanged from workload_identity_writer.go (#6061) alongside the existing batch-insert query fragments. It is a SQL string constant: it computes no state and performs no I/O on its own. Every writer that executes it (workload identity, cloud asset resolution, secrets/IAM trust chain, platform materialization, Kubernetes correlation, observability coverage correlation, and others) stays covered by `eshu_dp_postgres_query_duration_seconds` for the INSERT the row lands in, and `eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total` / `eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds` bound the reducer run around it -- the same trio the sibling factwrite rows cite. | reducer fact write |
| reducer shared projection intent shape | go/internal/reducer/sharedintent/intent.go | `No-Observability-Change:` Plain data shapes plus two pure functions that compute no state and perform no I/O; behavior-preserving hoist of the shared projection intent shape out of the reducer root (#6061). The shared_projection worker, runner, readiness, lease-heartbeat and batch-selection machinery that carries this domain's instrumentation all stays at the root and is unchanged, so the existing `eshu_dp_shared_edge_write_groups_total`, `eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total` and `eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds` coverage for shared projection is untouched. The intent rows this file builds are byte-identical to before: StableIntentID's derivation is pinned to an exact digest by sharedintent/intent_test.go, because it keys every intent already persisted in Postgres. | reducer shared projection |
| reducer shared projection context | go/internal/reducer/sharedintent/projection_context.go | `No-Observability-Change:` The bounded-unit freshness context a materializer stamps onto its intents, plus its pure acceptance-unit accessor. Behaviour-preserving hoist out of the reducer root (#6061); the body is unchanged and the root reaches it through an alias and a one-line forwarder, so no stage, trigger, queue write, retry, storage call, or graph write moved. This file holds no decode and emits nothing; the owning pass stays covered by `eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total` and `eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds` | reducer shared projection |
| reducer projection-context decode pass | go/internal/reducer/schemadecode/projection_context.go | `No-Observability-Change:` Builds one projection context per repository from a generation's repository facts. A repository fact that fails to decode, or that names no repository or source run, is skipped silently and increments no counter -- `eshu_dp_reducer_input_invalid_facts_total` is raised at the callers' file-fact quarantine, not here, so this read site surfaces only as a handler reporting success with `input_ready=0`. That gap is the accuracy hole issue #4749 targets and is unchanged by this move. The owning pass stays covered by `eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total` and `eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds` | reducer shared projection |
| reducer repo-refresh fence vocabulary | go/internal/reducer/sharedintent/refresh.go | `No-Observability-Change:` The repo-refresh intent type, action and retract-via-refresh marker, the whole-scope partition-key derivation, and the two pure functions that stamp a delta retract scope onto one repository's refresh payload; behavior-preserving hoist out of the reducer root (#6061). The bodies and the emitted strings are unchanged and the root reaches them through aliases and one-line forwarders, so the key the emitters build and the key the #2898 fence reconstructs still agree exactly. No stage, trigger, queue write, retry, storage call, or graph write moved; the shared projection cycle stays covered by `eshu_dp_shared_projection_cycles_total` and `eshu_dp_shared_projection_step_seconds`, and the edge writes it fences by `eshu_dp_shared_edge_write_groups_total` | reducer shared projection |
| reducer graph-projection keyspace enum | go/internal/reducer/gpphase/keyspace.go | `No-Observability-Change:` The `Keyspace` string enum and its 13 constants; behavior-preserving hoist of `GraphProjectionKeyspace` out of the reducer root's `graph_projection_phase.go` (#6061), unblocking the crossrepo family's remaining #6061 move. It computes no state and performs no I/O — the values are compared and persisted by the phase-repair machinery, which stays at the root untouched, and by the phase-publish machinery, whose builder and write wrapper (StateForIntentValue, PublishIntentGraphPhase) now live in this same package too (issue #6061, see the phasestate.go and phase_publish.go rows below) while the concrete publisher implementation (GraphProjectionPhaseStateStore, internal/storage/postgres/graph_projection_phase_state.go) stays unchanged at the root/postgres boundary, so `eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total` and `eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds` (the handler passes that read and publish these phases) and `eshu_dp_postgres_query_duration_seconds` (the Postgres reads/writes the publisher and repair queue issue) carry the same coverage as before. The root keeps every constant under its original `GraphProjectionKeyspace*` name as a const alias, so no caller changed. | reducer graph projection |
| reducer graph-projection phase key and readiness contract | go/internal/reducer/gpphase/phasekey.go | `No-Observability-Change:` The `Phase` string enum (7 constants), the `PhaseKey` bounded-slice identity struct plus its pure `Validate` method, and the `ReadinessLookup`/`ReadinessPrefetch` function types a domain family uses to read readiness; behavior-preserving hoist of `GraphProjectionPhase`, `GraphProjectionPhaseKey`, `GraphProjectionReadinessLookup`, and `GraphProjectionReadinessPrefetch` out of the reducer root (#6061) — a trial move of all five crossrepo-prefixed files confirmed these three (plus `GraphProjectionKeyspace`, in the sibling row above) were the family's only symbols with no existing leaf home; its other undefined names all resolved to already-hoisted sibling leaves (`sharedintent`, `contract`, `payloadcore`) by import or call-site rewrite alone. `Validate` computes no state and performs no I/O; the phase-repair machinery that actually retries a failed write stays at the root untouched, and the phase-publish machinery that actually writes readiness now lives partly in this package too (issue #6061; StateForIntentValue in phasestate.go, PublishIntentGraphPhase in phase_publish.go) while its concrete publisher implementation stays unchanged at the root/postgres boundary, so `eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total`, `eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds`, and `eshu_dp_postgres_query_duration_seconds` carry the same coverage as before. The root keeps every symbol under its original name as a type alias, so no caller changed. | reducer graph projection |
| reducer graph-projection readiness-key construction | go/internal/reducer/gpphase/key_from_scope.go | `No-Observability-Change:` KeyFromScope builds the bounded readiness identity (PhaseKey) from a scope generation, entity keys, and keyspace — the identity construction extracted from the root's graphProjectionPhaseStateForIntent (#6061) so a family that only reads readiness (internal/reducer/obscoverage) does not need the publish-and-write machinery a full state build requires. | deploy-event repository-mismatch skips (ci_cd_run correlation) | go/internal/reducer/cicdrun/ci_cd_run_correlation.go | `eshu_dp_cicd_deployment_events_skipped_total` (labels: domain, skip_reason=repository_mismatch) — counts ci.deployment_event facts LOST by the attach: rejected because the event and a sha-matching run named different repositories, and attached to no other run either. It counts lost facts rather than rejected (run, event) pairs, because an event fans out to every run sharing its sha by design and a pair count would multiply by that fan-out. This is the only signal for that condition: the drop is total for the affected run, the collector's deployment_unanchored ci.warning keys on sha rather than repository so it cannot fire, and validateTarget can only reject a PATH disagreement at startup because the run's repository html_url is unknown until collection — a host disagreement therefore reaches the reducer | reducer ci_cd_run correlation |

<!-- eshu:metric:section=projector-stages -->
## Projector Stages

The projector drains queue work items from `fact_work_items` (stage
`projector`) and writes source-local canonical graph nodes. It shares the
queue-depth and claim-wait surfaces with the reducer.

| stage | file:line | required metric name(s) | category |

| projection completion | go/internal/projector/service_logging.go:44 | `eshu_dp_projections_completed_total` | projector runtime |

Root assembly and enqueue remain covered by eshu_dp_reducer_intents_enqueued_total` | projector fact commit |

Unlike the scope-generation families this builder runs once per input fact inside root buildProjection, so it can return one intent per accepted fact; all of them carry the same repo:<repo_id> entity key and collapse to one claimable reducer work item. The source-system label stays the raw SourceRef.SourceSystem (no two-tier projectorintent.SourceSystem fallback), the pre-move behavior. Intent volume remains covered by eshu_dp_reducer_intents_enqueued_total and eshu_dp_projector_run_duration_seconds; the reducer execution that consumes the intent is covered by eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total and eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds; this file emits no metric of its own` | projector fact commit |

Root assembly and enqueue remain covered by eshu_dp_reducer_intents_enqueued_total` | projector fact commit |
| AWS cloud-image materialization intent trigger | go/internal/projector/awscloudimage/materialization_intents.go | `No-Observability-Change: enqueues one reducer.DomainAWSCloudImageMaterialization intent per scope generation carrying an aws_resource fact (AWSResourceFactKind), NOT gated on the lambda_function_uses_image relationship fact — an Image-to-Zip Lambda transition drops that relationship fact yet must still enqueue so the handler can retract the prior edge, so operators reading eshu_dp_reducer_intents_enqueued_total should treat that reconciliation enqueue as required, not anomalous; runs through the existing projector intent path; intent volume is covered by eshu_dp_reducer_intents_enqueued_total and eshu_dp_projector_run_duration_seconds, and the reducer execution that consumes the intent is covered by eshu_dp_aws_cloud_image_edges_total plus eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total / eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds (see the aws_cloud_image_materialization.go row above); this pure intent builder emits no metric of its own` | projector fact commit |
| AWS cloud-runtime-drift intent trigger | go/internal/projector/awscloudruntimedrift/reducer_intent.go | `No-Observability-Change: behavior-preserving package move (#6057) of the aws_resource-presence trigger and its two-tier trimmed source-system label (SourceRef.SourceSystem, else CollectorKind, checked body-for-body against projectorintent.SourceSystem and found identical, so the local helper was dropped rather than moved, and its two other root callers repointed to projectorintent.SourceSystem in the same change); root assembly and eshu_dp_reducer_intents_enqueued_total remain unchanged. This domain is covered by the root fan-out parity fixture. Intent volume is covered by eshu_dp_reducer_intents_enqueued_total and eshu_dp_projector_run_duration_seconds; the reducer's bounded ARN join is covered by the reducer.aws_runtime_drift_evidence_load span and eshu_dp_postgres_query_duration_seconds, and admitted findings by eshu_dp_correlation_orphan_detected_total / eshu_dp_correlation_unmanaged_detected_total; this pure intent builder emits no metric of its own` | projector fact commit |
| AWS resource-materialization intent trigger | go/internal/projector/awsresource/materialization_intents.go | `No-Observability-Change: behavior-preserving package move (#6057) of the aws_resource-presence trigger for reducer.DomainAWSResourceMaterialization (#805). The pre-extraction root file already called the shared two-tier projectorintent.SourceSystem seam directly, so no helper was substituted or duplicated; trigger kind, FirstOfKind anchor, reason string, and the aws_resource_materialization:<scope> entity key are byte-identical, and only the scope/generation identifiers changed from struct-field reads to string parameters. That entity key is the shared AWS acceptance unit eleven other reducer-intent builders emit, and internal/storage/postgres hashes its prefix into the cloud-resource-node queue conflict family for this domain only -- reducerCloudResourceNodeConflictKey applies to a domain whose resource-conflict policy is marked safe, which today is DomainAWSResourceMaterialization alone, while the sibling AWS families group by resource_scope or the default -- so operators reading queue conflict groupings see no change either. This domain is covered by the root fan-out parity fixture. Intent volume is covered by eshu_dp_reducer_intents_enqueued_total and eshu_dp_projector_run_duration_seconds; the reducer execution that consumes the intent by eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total and eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds, its CloudResource node write is NOT covered by eshu_dp_canonical_writes_total (AWSResourceMaterializationHandler returns Result.CanonicalWrites but Service.recordReducerResult never forwards it, and the handler is not one of the five CanonicalWrites.Add call sites) -- the written node count and graph-write duration are carried by the `aws resource materialization completed` structured log; this pure intent builder emits no metric of its own` | projector fact commit |
| AWS EC2 instance identity materialization intent trigger | go/internal/projector/ec2/instance_identity_materialization_intents.go | `No-Observability-Change: the child ec2 package's builder returns one reducer.DomainEC2InstanceIdentityMaterialization intent per scope generation carrying an ec2_instance_posture fact (EC2InstancePostureFactKind) — the SAME fact DomainEC2InstanceNodeMaterialization triggers on, so this domain enqueues exactly when the EC2 instance node it augments (and its readiness gate waits on) does. Triggering on a generic aws_resource fact was the #5743 residual bug: a no-EC2-instance aws scope (ecr/lambda/ecs) got an intent whose readiness gate could never open, leaving the work item stuck 'pending'. The handler loads the co-present aws_ec2_instance aws_resource fact for the ami_id; a scope with the node but no matching row is a safe no-op. Root assembly appends the returned intent and Runtime.Project enqueues it; intent volume is covered by eshu_dp_reducer_intents_enqueued_total and eshu_dp_projector_run_duration_seconds, and the reducer execution that consumes the intent by eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total / eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds; the child builder emits no metric of its own` | projector fact commit |
| Kubernetes namespace materialization intent trigger | go/internal/projector/kubernetes/namespace_materialization_intents.go | `No-Observability-Change: the child builder returns one reducer.DomainKubernetesNamespaceMaterialization intent per namespace-bearing or complete Kubernetes live cluster generation, including complete empty snapshots for absent-node reconciliation; partial empty snapshots return no intent. Root assembly appends returned intents, and Runtime.Project enqueues the assembled slice through IntentWriter.Enqueue. Intent volume remains covered by eshu_dp_reducer_intents_enqueued_total and eshu_dp_projector_run_duration_seconds; reducer execution and canonical node/environment-edge upserts remain covered by eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total, eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds, and eshu_dp_canonical_writes_total. The reducer completion evidence and log expose reconciliation_status=not_requested|applied|suppressed_input_invalid so an empty successful sweep is distinguishable from no reconciliation; the child builder emits no metric of its own` | projector fact commit |
| CI/CD run correlation intent trigger | go/internal/projector/cicdruncorrelation/correlation_intents.go | `No-Observability-Change: behavior-preserving package move of the ci.run / ci.artifact trigger (a run always outranks a same-generation artifact, two independent FirstOfKind probes, #5710) and its two-tier trimmed source-system label (SourceRef.SourceSystem, else CollectorKind, checked body-for-body against projectorintent.SourceSystem and found identical); root assembly and eshu_dp_reducer_intents_enqueued_total remain unchanged. A run starts the existing full-snapshot path. An artifact without a co-located run starts #5770's bounded patch path: the reducer rebuilds the newest older run-window snapshot from source evidence, unions exact patch keys, applies current-generation live and tombstone controls, and writes the full target-generation snapshot without depending on an older reducer result. Intent volume remains covered by eshu_dp_reducer_intents_enqueued_total and eshu_dp_projector_run_duration_seconds; the reducer execution and its historical Postgres read remain covered by eshu_dp_ci_cd_run_correlations_total, eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total, eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds, and eshu_dp_postgres_query_duration_seconds. The builder emits no metric of its own` | projector fact commit |
| CI/CD artifact-only correlation patch | go/internal/reducer/cicdrun/ci_cd_run_correlation_patch.go | `No-New-Metric: detects an artifact-only generation, extracts exact provider/run/attempt keys from live artifacts, asks FactStore for the newest older run-window baseline plus those live keys, applies current non-artifact facts by exact typed stable identity, removes valid current tombstones as stable-key control rows, and reclassifies the bounded patch snapshot before the existing writer publishes it (#5770). Queue supersession cannot drop unaffected runs, runs and ancillary evidence omitted by a newer normal window stay absent, and current tombstones never seed omitted runs. Missing loader support, read failures, blank tombstone identity, and the 1,000-decision or 12,000-fact caps return through the existing reducer failure/dead-letter path. Enqueue and execution remain covered by eshu_dp_reducer_intents_enqueued_total, eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total, eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds, eshu_dp_ci_cd_run_correlations_total, and eshu_dp_postgres_query_duration_seconds; this helper adds no metric of its own` | reducer ci_cd_run correlation |
| CI/CD retained run-history evidence read | go/internal/storage/postgres/facts_cicd_run_history.go | `No-Observability-Change: FactStore reads either exact provider/run/attempt history or #5770's newest older normal run-window baseline unioned with exact live patch keys. Scope-patch reads keep baseline and later evidence, allow a live artifact to recover only an older ci.run anchor, rank tombstones before filtering, and reload repository-matched workflow-image evidence plus deployment events only inside the authoritative window. The separate exact-history API retains payload-empty artifact tombstone routing for callers that request it; the scope-patch reducer does not use tombstones as run keys. The query rejects more than 12,000 facts, uses the existing instrumented Postgres adapter and eshu_dp_postgres_query_duration_seconds, and reports handler failures through eshu_dp_reducer_executions_total and eshu_dp_reducer_run_duration_seconds. This storage file registers no metric or label` | reducer storage |

## Collector Dispatch Seams

Every collector family runs under the shared claimed-service worker harness
(`go/internal/collector/claimed_service.go`). One row per dispatch chokepoint
covers all collector families; per-collector volume counters and durations
land at the same call sites.

| git-tracked file resolution (issue #5591) | go/internal/collector/gitrepo/git_tracked_files.go | `eshu_dp_discovery_files_skipped_total` (labeled `skip_reason=eshuignore`): a file git tracks is never git-ignored, so a force-committed (`git add -f`) file that matches a .gitignore rule no longer counts toward this metric's `skip_reason=gitignore` label at all — .eshuignore is unaffected and still increments `skip_reason=eshuignore` for a tracked file the operator explicitly excluded. That specific tracked-file eshuignore skip also gets a dedicated per-path "tracked file skipped by eshuignore" structured log (emitted from git_snapshot_discovery.go's logDiscoveryStats and git_selection_filesystem.go's copyRepositoryTree) and a count in DiscoveryAdvisoryReport.SkipBreakdown.TrackedFilesEshuIgnore, so operators can see which tracked files were deliberately kept out of the index | collector discovery |
| discovery filesystem walk (issue #5591 file-cap split) | go/internal/collector/discovery/filesystem_walk.go | No-Observability-Change: pure filesystem-walk helpers (collectSupportedFiles, groupFilesByRepository, nearestRepositoryRoot, classifyPath, and their supporting normalize/match helpers) split out of discovery.go verbatim to stay under the repo's 500-line file cap after the #5591 GitTrackedResolver addition — no behavior change; still covered by the same `eshu_dp_discovery_dirs_skipped_total`/`eshu_dp_discovery_files_skipped_total` metrics as the "discovery" row above | collector discovery |
| discovery advisory report shape | go/internal/collector/advisory_report.go | No-Observability-Change: the `DiscoveryAdvisoryReport` type and its six companion structs, split verbatim out of `discovery_advisory.go` (#6056) so the collector seam keeps the shape `CollectedGeneration.DiscoveryAdvisory` carries while the builder follows the git collector into `gitrepo`; type declarations only, no statements and no instrumentation, and the advisory is not persisted with facts — the builder that populates it stays covered by `eshu_dp_discovery_files_skipped_total` (git-tracked file resolution row above) | collector discovery |
| filesystem managed-copy tracked resolution (issue #5658) | go/internal/collector/gitrepo/git_selection_tracked.go | No-Observability-Change: the collectorTrackedResolver is a lazy, memoized, nearest-git-root git-tracked-set resolver for copyRepositoryTree's managed-copy walk (issue #5658 P1a/P1b); it emits no metric of its own — its only runtime signal is the warnGitTrackedFilesUnavailable WARN (defined in git_tracked_files.go, covered by the "git-tracked file resolution" row above) and the same per-path "tracked file skipped by eshuignore" structured log plus DiscoveryAdvisoryReport.SkipBreakdown.TrackedFilesEshuIgnore count already covered by that row | collector discovery |
| filesystem managed-copy commit binding | go/internal/collector/gitrepo/git_selection_copy_commit.go | No-Observability-Change: loads immutable Git blob identities once and hashes each admitted regular file while streaming the same bytes into the managed copy before carrying source_commit_sha. Missing commit paths are allowed for observed collector-policy skips, while changed, extra, submodule, unreadable, Git-error, clean-filter, encoding, and line-ending-transform cases fail closed. The operation runs only for a changed clean filesystem managed-copy repository inside selector execution, covered by the existing scope.assign span and eshu_dp_scope_assign_duration_seconds plus the collector discovery completed structured log and durable scope_generations.source_commit_sha; this helper emits no metric, label, span, or log field of its own | collector per-collector |
| filesystem managed-copy Unix source opening | go/internal/collector/gitrepo/git_selection_copy_open_unix.go | No-Observability-Change: keeps one root descriptor and only the current walk ancestry pinned, then uses no-follow nonblocking openat plus pre/open/post identity checks so symlink swaps cannot copy excluded or outside-root bytes. Existing scope.assign timing and collector discovery completion signals cover this local copy work; this helper emits no metric, label, span, or log field of its own | collector per-collector |
| filesystem managed-copy Windows source opening | go/internal/collector/gitrepo/git_selection_copy_open_windows.go | No-Observability-Change: confines relative opens through os.Root and relies on the shared pre/open/post identity checks before bytes are read. Existing scope.assign timing and collector discovery completion signals cover this local copy work; this helper emits no metric, label, span, or log field of its own | collector per-collector |

| awscloud typed-depth extractor registry | go/internal/collector/awscloud/extractor.go | No-Observability-Change: declares the per-resource-type extractor registry (`RegisterResourceExtractor`, `extractResourceAttributes`), mirroring `go/internal/collector/gcpcloud/extractor.go`. NOTHING is registered and nothing calls it, so it is not on any emission path: an unregistered resource type returns `handled=false` with no error and the caller keeps its bounded base observation unchanged. Its producer is the AWS Config lane (#6088), NOT the service scanners -- scanners hold typed SDK structs rather than the raw per-resource JSON this registry dispatches on, and they already build typed depth directly into a `ResourceObservation`; routing one through here would change no fact output. When the Config lane registers extractors, its emission volume and duration are covered by the same signals AWS collection uses today -- `eshu_dp_aws_resources_emitted_total` and `eshu_dp_aws_scan_duration_seconds` (AWS cloud row, go/internal/collector/awscloud/awsruntime/source.go:177) plus `eshu_dp_facts_emitted_total` and `eshu_dp_facts_committed_total` (fact commit row, go/internal/collector/gitrepo/git_source_processing.go:217) -- because extraction fills attributes on the existing observation path rather than changing what is emitted or how it is committed; this file emits no metric of its own | collector per-collector |

## Parser Language Sub-Packages

Every parser emits per-file facts through the shared `FactEmitDuration` and
`FileParseDuration` surfaces at the parser entry point
(`go/internal/collector/gitrepo/git_snapshot_native.go`). Per-language structured
events land through the `evidence_facts_discovered_total` counter when the
parser emits evidence facts.

<!-- eshu:metric:section=queue-domains -->
## Queue Domains

The Postgres `fact_work_items` table holds two stages (`projector`, `reducer`);
a separate `semantic_extraction_jobs` table holds provider semantic jobs.
Each domain has a depth/age gauge pair sourced from the queue observer.

| stage | file:line | required metric name(s) | category |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| reducer queue | go/internal/storage/postgres/queue_observer.go:112 | `eshu_dp_queue_depth`, `eshu_dp_queue_oldest_age_seconds`, `eshu_dp_queue_source_depth`, `eshu_dp_queue_source_oldest_age_seconds`, `eshu_dp_workflow_family_queue_depth` | queue domain |
| projector queue | go/internal/storage/postgres/queue_observer.go:112 (stage=projector) | `eshu_dp_queue_depth`, `eshu_dp_queue_oldest_age_seconds`, `eshu_dp_queue_source_depth`, `eshu_dp_queue_source_oldest_age_seconds`, `eshu_dp_workflow_family_queue_depth` | queue domain |
| semantic extraction queue | go/internal/storage/postgres/queue_observer.go:57 (semanticQueueDepthQuery) | `eshu_dp_queue_depth` | queue domain |
| worker pool active | go/internal/telemetry/instruments.go:3617 | `eshu_dp_worker_pool_active` | queue runtime |
| workflow claim lease age | go/internal/collector/claimed_service_backpressure_metrics.go:78 | `eshu_dp_workflow_claim_lease_age_seconds` | queue runtime |
| go runtime memory limit | go/cmd/ingester/main.go:61 | `eshu_dp_gomemlimit_bytes` | queue runtime |
| reducer graph-write-timeout retrying | go/internal/storage/postgres/queue_observer.go:168 | `eshu_dp_queue_depth`, `eshu_dp_graph_write_backpressure_engaged_total` | queue runtime |

| status stage-counts cache | go/internal/storage/postgres/status_stage_counts_cache.go:121 (listStageCounts) | `eshu_dp_status_stage_counts_cache_total` | status query cache |

backpressure executor and failure classifier can preserve phase ordering and
diagnose failure causes. Each row maps a canonical phase to its metric.

<!-- eshu:metric:section=mcp-api-routes -->
## MCP / API Routes

Every query API and MCP read route emits two metrics through one middleware
(`go/internal/query/request_metrics.go:75`). One row covers the entire route
catalog; per-route variants share the same `route` label dimension.

| stage | file:line | required metric name(s) | category |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HTTP API/MCP per-route latency | go/internal/query/request_metrics.go:75 | `eshu_dp_api_request_duration_seconds`, `eshu_dp_api_request_errors_total` | query surface |
| HTTP API/MCP per-route span | go/internal/query/queryspan/handlerspan.go:48 | `eshu_dp_api_request_duration_seconds` (parent), `query.*` span | query surface |
| Graph-read deadline and retry policy | go/internal/query/neo4j_read_policy.go | `eshu_dp_neo4j_query_duration_seconds` (`operation="read"`; bounded `outcome`: `success`, `slow`, `recovered`, `deadline`, `caller_deadline`, `unavailable`, `canceled`, or `error`), `neo4j.query` span, sanitized `query.graph_read.warning` log | query graph |
| Cloud resource owner-ledger upgrade backfill | go/internal/query/cloud_resource_owner_backfill.go | `No-Observability-Change: one-time API/MCP startup migration before the route is mounted; each graph page uses the shared bounded graph-read policy and carries its neo4j.query span, completion emits the structured "cloud resource owner ledger backfill complete" log with pages_seeded, rows_seeded, and duration_seconds, startup errors fail the process with context, and steady-state requests retain eshu_dp_cloud_resource_list_duration_seconds` | query cloud |
| Cloud resources (bounded page selection and hydration) | go/internal/query/cloud_resources.go:58 | `eshu_dp_api_request_duration_seconds`, `eshu_dp_api_request_errors_total`, `eshu_dp_cloud_resource_list_duration_seconds`, `eshu_dp_cloud_resource_list_errors_total`, `eshu_dp_cloud_resource_list_scanned_rows` (logical owner-ledger candidates returned by the bounded `limit+1` selection), `eshu_dp_cloud_resource_list_page_size`, `eshu_dp_cloud_resource_list_truncations_total` | query cloud |
| IaC resources | go/internal/query/iac_resources.go | `eshu_dp_iac_resource_list_duration_seconds`, `eshu_dp_iac_resource_list_errors_total`, `eshu_dp_api_request_duration_seconds` | query IaC |

## Diff Semantics

- The CI coverage script (X2) reads this doc as a sequence of
  `stage | file:line | metric | category` rows. It parses every line that
  starts with `|` after a section header.
- For each row, the script asserts at least one of:
  1. The metric column lists a registered instrument name from
     `go/internal/telemetry/instruments.go` (X2 grep's the `meter.Int64Counter`,
     `meter.Float64Histogram`, etc., declarations for that exact name).
  2. The metric column contains the literal `No-Observability-Change:` token
     followed by a justification naming the existing signal.
- For each row whose `file:line` references a Go file, the script asserts
  that the file exists and the line range contains a recording call
  (`Instruments.*.Record`, `Instruments.*.Add`, `s.Instruments.*.Record`,
  `s.Instruments.*.Add`, `tracer.Start`, or `slog.*`).
- For each row whose category is `cypher phase`, the script asserts that the
  phase string is registered in `go/internal/storage/cypher/phase_group_metadata.go`.
- A new pipeline stage added to the source tree without a corresponding row
  in this doc fails the X2 + X3 gate. The error message names the missing
  row's expected location.
- Maintainers update this doc in the same PR that adds the new stage. The
  doc is hand-authored; X2 does not auto-generate rows from source code.

## Markers

- `No-Observability-Change:` is a literal string the script grep-parses for.
  It appears in the metric column when the stage does not need a new metric
  because an existing one already diagnoses it.
- The marker is per-stage: one marker per row that does not have a registered
  metric name. The script treats the absence of a registered metric AND the
  absence of a marker as a gate failure.
- The marker is NOT a "I forgot". It is a documented decision that names the
  existing signal and why it covers the stage. Example:
  `No-Observability-Change: git-source collector metrics (FactsEmitted, RepoSnapshotDuration) cover preflight throughput`.
- The marker language matches the `No-Observability-Change:` evidence marker
  in the per-PR commit message policy at
  `docs/internal/agent-guide.md:143`. PR authors cite the same row in their
  commit message.

## Regeneration Note

- This doc is hand-authored. There is no auto-generation from
  `go/internal/telemetry/instruments.go` or any other source file.
- Each row is one stage. The table is the source of truth for X2.
- To add a new stage:
  1. Register the metric in `go/internal/telemetry/instruments.go` (or
     confirm an existing instrument already diagnoses the path).
  2. Add a row to the appropriate section of this doc, including the
     `file:line` citation to the Go file that records the metric.
  3. Run `wc -l docs/public/observability/telemetry-coverage.md` to confirm
     the doc stays under the 500-line file rule.
  4. Run the docs build:
     `uv run --with mkdocs --with mkdocs-material --with pymdown-extensions \
     mkdocs build --strict --clean --config-file docs/mkdocs.yml`.
  5. Run `git diff --check` to confirm no whitespace errors.
- To remove a stage, delete the row and remove the matching metric from
  `go/internal/telemetry/instruments.go` in the same PR. X2 will fail
  otherwise because the metric is still registered.

## Linked Work

- [#3633](https://github.com/eshu-hq/eshu/issues/3633) (closed 2026-06-23) —
  root-cause class: defined-but-never-registered instruments. The historical
  precedent that motivated this contract.
- [#3680](https://github.com/eshu-hq/eshu/issues/3680) (open, 2026-06-24,
  closed at the time of #3694 sign-off) — per-collector envelope telemetry.
  The in-flight adoption that adds the per-collector rows above.
- [`go/internal/telemetry/instruments.go`](https://github.com/eshu-hq/eshu/blob/main/go/internal/telemetry/instruments.go)
  — metric source of truth. Every metric name in the tables above resolves to
  a `meter.*(...)` declaration here.
- [`go/internal/telemetry/contract.go`](https://github.com/eshu-hq/eshu/blob/main/go/internal/telemetry/contract.go)
  — dimension keys, span names, log keys. Every span name in the OTEL Span
  Names section resolves to a `Span*` constant here or in the sibling
  `contract_*.go` files.
- [`docs/public/reference/telemetry/index.md`](https://github.com/eshu-hq/eshu/blob/main/docs/public/reference/telemetry/index.md)
  — public operator contract. This coverage doc extends the public contract
  by enumerating every stage, not just the most-used signals.
- [`docs/internal/agent-guide.md:120-146`](https://github.com/eshu-hq/eshu/blob/main/docs/internal/agent-guide.md)
  — five evidence markers policy. The marker language in the doc and PR
  commits comes from this section.

## Flow Affected

`reducer -> graph write -> telemetry contract`. This is the flow the contract
covers. New reducer projection stages, new shared-edge writers, and new
graph-write statement phases all flow through this contract; the X2 script
is the machine-enforced gate.

Bucket sets for `eshu_dp_cloud_resource_list_*` are defined in
`go/internal/query/cloud_resources_metrics.go`, next to the code that emits
them, rather than in `go/internal/telemetry/instruments.go`. The table above
covers instruments registered in `instruments.go`, which is what the bucket
check reads, so those sets are deliberately absent from it (#5548).
